## 0. The rulings this unit derives from (recorded, NOT re-opened)

1. **THE RULING, AND ITS FIVE ANSWERS** (`A`; `docs/decisions.md`'s row **`TIER-4 READS RETURN DEEP CLONES OVER THE FULL READ SURFACE, AND TIER-4 WRITE SURFACES ARE ADMISSION-CONTROLLED BY THEIR REACHABLE SET — `D-1` · `D-2`**, taken on the gate-1 record's amended verdict `APPROVE-WITH-CONDITIONS`): the architect's words, verbatim — *"Data requests from the security do not return references to original data, they return clones to prevent external read/mutation by reference. Writes at runtime are permitted iff the MCP is blocked"* — with the five answers at the gate-1 record `§12.2`: **(1)** `D-2` is **STRUCTURAL** (the write surfaces stay non-MCP-reachable; enforcement lives at the exclusion gate's invocation turn) · **(2)** the clone discipline is a **DEEP** copy, cycle-safe and prototype-safe, **AND any value the tier cannot persist as JSON MUST NOT BE ACCEPTED** — the precedent the answer names is the landed `snapshotValue` (`L` `src/renderer/store-core-graph.ts:362-388`: a `seen` map, `Object.create(null)`, no depth bound) — and **a JSON round-trip is NOT the implementation of either half** (it throws on cycles/`BigInt`), so **the admission test is a VALUE-PRESERVATION test on the write path** · **(3)** **the read-surface set is the FULL access surface — NO carve-out** (the measured census `["get","lastWriteReceipt","set"]`; `lastWriteReceipt()` is **IN** the set, a fix obligation with its own red row) · **(4)** **a write is not treated as fully committed until it successfully saves to file** · **(5)** "see above" — (2)/(3)/(4) extend to the **boot-ingestion reads and the persist path**, and an unrepresentable write must not be committed.
2. **THE FOLLOW-UP RULING — READING (b), AND IT IS THE AUTHORITY FOR `O-1`** (`A`; the same row's **dated follow-up block**, `2026-10-11`, an insertion beside the row, whose as-filed bytes stand): the architect's answer, verbatim — ***"Live state equals durable state"*** — i.e. **reading (b)**: a refused persist **ROLLS THE RECORD BACK**, so a read never shows a value the file does not carry; **`current` is assigned ONLY after a SUCCESSFUL `persist()`, and a refusal RETURNS THE PRE-WRITE RECORD**; **the receipt and the tier AGREE**; **the pane renders the PRE-WRITE state and never an uncommitted one**. **This block governs the reading of the row above wherever the two are read together, and it is the clause `O-1` implements.**
3. **THE THREE MEASURED DEFECTS, AND THEIR DISPOSITIONS** (`A`/`M`; `docs/defects.md`'s three `## OPEN` rows + the dated disposition block beneath them): **`SECURITY-STORE-RECORD-ADVANCES-PAST-A-REFUSED-PERSIST`** — **DECIDED IN FAVOUR OF ROLLBACK** (arm (a) of that row's own fix shape: the `current` assignment moves AFTER a successful `persist()`, and a refusal returns the PRE-WRITE record); **`SECURITY-STORE-UNREPRESENTABLE-SETTING-PERSISTS-AS-NULL`** — **GOVERNED BY THE RULING'S JSON-REPRESENTABILITY ADMISSION**, *"any value the tier cannot persist as JSON must not be accepted"*; **`SECURITY-STORE-COMMITTED-RECEIPT-ON-A-DROPPED-VALUE`** — **GOVERNED BY THE SAME ADMISSION** (no `committed` receipt may answer a value the write discarded). **`O-3`'s defect is the gate-1 record's own `§8 F-1` → `§12.5` `CP-2` → `§12.8` `F-11`** (a fix obligation with its own red row, *not* a semantics gap).
4. **THIS ROW'S ADMISSION AUTHORITY** (`A`; `RCA-8(f)` — *the architect admits ROWS, not a pass*): the architect's directive **"Proceed with repair"** is the admission of row **`S3`**; the row is recorded in `docs/next-steps.md`'s `## OPEN` ledger with its arithmetic by the filing pass. **This unit is admitted as a DEFECT-REPAIR unit and not as a new scope:** its three obligations are already-decided clauses of the tier-4 ruling, and it opens no new question, no new surface and no new identifier.
5. **GATE 1 IS DISCHARGED FOR THESE CLAUSES** (`A`/`CROSS-REF`; the gate-1 record `docs/specs/tier4-clone-and-write-gate-review.md` `§12` AMENDMENT ONE, verdict re-issued `APPROVE-WITH-CONDITIONS` at `§12.6`): the five `must-fix` questions are answered, so the STEP-0 bar they raised is discharged and the as-filed `BLOCKED-ON-SEMANTICS` no longer stands. **The chain that produced this record is the one these clauses ride: validity → critique → architecture review → change-analysis (`AGENTS.md` item 8's step-4 gate record), run on the ruling.** `§12.7`'s line does NOT move by this unit: `S2` remains behind `S1`'s DONE row.
6. **THE LANDED CLONE PRECEDENT — THE SHAPE `O-3` ADOPTS, AND THE ROUND-TRIP THAT IS INADMISSIBLE** (`A`/`L`; answers (2)/(3); `L` `src/renderer/store-core-graph.ts:362-388`): `snapshotValue` is the named precedent: a **`seen` map for cycles**, **`Object.create(null)` for prototypes**, **no depth bound**, and **no aliasing at any depth**. **A JSON round-trip is inadmissible as the implementation of either half of the ruling** — it throws on cycles and `BigInt`, and it can poison prototypes in a security store.
7. **THE RECEIPT'S HOME AND ITS CLOSED FORM ARE UNMOVED** (`A`/`S`; the `D-GATE` row's clause (2); `docs/specs/store-security.md` `§0A` item 3 + `§2.1` item 5): `SecurityWriteReceipt = { status: 'committed' } | { status: 'refused'; reason: 'write-failed' }` — **TWO closed forms, ONE reason token**, deliberately **NOT a member of the store's closed 16-member refusal union**; **a second security token added to that union is a COLLISION finding**, and **the union stays `16`** (`S` `store-core-graph.md` `§2.1`'s block annotation). **This unit adds NO third form, NO second refusal union and NO new token** — the whole new refusal traffic rides the landed two-form union (`§0A` item 1).
8. **THE CARRY THE `docs/defects.md` DISPOSITION LEFT OPEN IS RESOLVED BY THIS ADMISSION** (`CROSS-REF`; that file's dated disposition block): *"whether these three (all in `src/main/security-store.ts` …) are `S2`'s or a separate row"* — **the architect's directive answers it: they are THIS row's (`S3`), and `S2`'s boundary is not opened** (`§5.1`). The defect rows are **not closed by this filing**; they close at this unit's landing (`§5.7` item 4).

### 0A. The dated ruling notes — the decisions THIS unit makes as working defaults, each with its reversibility (none blocks)

1. **THE REFUSAL'S FORM FOR AN UNREPRESENTABLE VALUE — THE LANDED CLOSED RECEIPT, WHOLE-PATCH, WITH ITS REASON TOKEN'S COVERAGE EXTENDED** (`T`, `CONTRACT` + `DECLARED-DEFAULT` on the token's COVERAGE; `§7c` `Q-1` is the one `undefined-until-answered` question this filing records, and this is its declared default). An unrepresentable patch member **REFUSES THE WHOLE PATCH**: the receipt is the landed `{ status: 'refused', reason: 'write-failed' }`, the record does not move, and **the reason token's declared coverage grows by ONE named site — the ADMISSION CHECK that precedes the staging write** — recorded as a dated annotation beside `docs/specs/store-security.md` `§2.3` item 5 at this unit's landing (the landed comment says the token covers "the tmp-write, the fsync and the rename failure points"; it now covers those three **plus the admission check**). **WHY NOT A NEW TOKEN, stated so nothing is silent:** a distinct reason member would be a second refusal vocabulary inside the security receipt — the class `F-6`/the `D-GATE` row's clause (2) close — and the requirement (`A` answer (2): *"must not be accepted"*) is satisfied by the refusal itself. **THE PRICED ALTERNATIVE, NOT TAKEN, AND THE ARCHITECT'S TO RULE:** `docs/defects.md`'s `SECURITY-STORE-COMMITTED-RECEIPT-ON-A-DROPPED-VALUE` row proposes *"a third CLOSED form (e.g. `{status:'refused', reason:'unrepresentable-value'}`) or a `dropped: [...]` member"*; **either is a one-cell amendment to this item plus the receipt rows of the red set** (`§2.3` item 4), and **neither extends the STORE's 16-member union** (the receipt is a channel vocabulary — the reason it is not a union member at all). **REVERSIBLE BY THE ARCHITECT AT THIS SPEC GATE; a reversal is `§2.1` item 6 + `§3.2` `F-2`/`F-4`'s receipt-shape rows, never a silent change.**
2. **THE COMMIT POINT IS THE SUCCESSFUL RENAME, AND A POST-COMMIT DIRECTORY-`fsync` FAILURE DOES NOT ROLL BACK** (`T`, `CONTRACT`; the reading that keeps `O-1` coherent at the one failure point the rename does not cover). **⟶ ANNOTATED BESIDE, DATED `2026-10-11` (THE POST-DONE AMENDMENT PASS; `A` for the ruling, `T` for the consequence; `RCA-8(d)`: every as-filed byte of this item below STANDS and is not rewritten — this note is inserted beside its ORDER sentence).** **THE ORDER SENTENCE IS NOW THE OPERATIVE AND REQUIRED ORDER.** The architect's answer to this unit's residual ORDER question — *"Lock out the reads until the commit lands"* — is landed as **`§2.6`** (THE WRITE LOCK, appended), whose items 4/5 pin the consequence: **the record's advance must LAND AT THE COMMIT (the successful `renameSync`) and the read surface must flip there**, so this item's *"ordered after the successful `renameSync` … and before the parent-directory `fsyncSync`"* is no longer merely declared-but-unobservable — it is an OBSERVABLE requirement, driven at the post-rename probe. **WHAT THIS NOTE DOES *NOT* CHANGE:** the second half of this item is UNTOUCHED and is not qualified by `§2.6` — **a post-rename directory-`fsync` failure is still NOT a refusal, the write stays `committed`, the record stays advanced, and nothing rolls back** (`§2.6` item 5's precedence clause (ii) says so explicitly). **A reversal of either half is an architect's act; a pass may neither drop the landing requirement nor re-open the dir-`fsync` terminal.** The record's advance is ordered **after the successful `renameSync`** (`§2.1` item 3's step 4) and **before** the parent-directory `fsyncSync`, because **the file's CONTENT is already the new record from the moment the rename lands** — so a post-rename directory-`fsync` failure that rolled the record back would put the live record and the file's bytes **into disagreement, which is the exact thing reading (b) forbids**. The declared answer: **the write is `committed`, the record advances, and live equals durable**; the durability strengthening (the directory `fsyncSync`, which orders the rename's own metadata against a power loss) is **best-effort at that point**, and its failure **is not a refusal and moves nothing back**. **THE `G3` PRECEDENT THIS AMENDS, NAMED:** `docs/specs/store-security.md` `§2.2` item 6 and its `P-SE-TP-1`/`P-SE-SM-1` terms treat **all four** injection points (tmp-write · fsync · rename · dir-fsync) as refusals with the previous record intact; **the `G3` gate-4 `NEW-2` disposition already TIGHTENED the dir-fsync arm's wording** (its refused terminal was re-aimed to *"the commit-side refusal (the refused receipt + the NEW record live)"*). **This item states the coherent form of that re-aim: at the dir-fsync point the record is LIVE, and the answer is `committed`.** **REVERSIBLE BY THE ARCHITECT AT THIS SPEC GATE; a reversal to "dir-fsync failure ⇒ refusal" is a one-cell amendment to this item plus `§2.1` item 3 and `§3.1` `M-2`'s ordering step — AND IT RE-OPENS THE LIVE-VERSUS-DURABLE DISAGREEMENT, which is stated here rather than discovered later.**
3. **THE ADMISSION'S POSITION — BEFORE THE STAGING WRITE, DERIVED FROM THE CALLER, AND THE CANDIDATE IS A LOCAL** (`T`, `CONTRACT`): the admission check runs **before `persist()` touches the filesystem**, and `persist()` is re-parameterised to receive the **candidate** record as an argument (never the closure's `current`) — so a refusal at ANY point leaves `current` untouched by construction, not by ordering luck. **A refusal that has already written the staging file is not a failure of this clause (the refusal still returns the pre-write record); but an ADMISSION refusal that touches the filesystem at all is a FAILING row** (`§5.6.1` `P-O2-TP-1`'s attempt (3)), because the value never entered the write path. **REVERSIBLE by a one-cell amendment; the alternative (admit after staging) is priced as strictly worse — it makes the value's fate depend on the filesystem's availability.**
4. **THE CLONE'S MECHANICS — `Object.create(null)` + a `seen` map, AND THE RECEIPT'S COPY IS A PLAIN OBJECT LITERAL** (`T`, `CONTRACT`; `§0` ruling 6). Every value this module RETURNS from a declared member is **detached at every depth, cycle-safe and prototype-safe**: the copy is built with **`Object.create(null)`** as its prototype and a **`seen` map** as its cycle guard — the `snapshotValue` shape, not a round-trip. **THE RECEIPT'S COPY IS DECLARED AS A PLAIN OBJECT LITERAL** (`{ ... }` whose members are the two primitives `status`/`reason`), **not** a null-prototype object, because **the receipt is a value THIS module authors and no caller value reaches it** — so there is no prototype hazard to guard, and a null-prototype receipt would only make the returned record's own shape drift from the landed `SecurityWriteReceipt` for no gain. **`get()`'s record, `get()`'s `enabled` array, and any array/object inside a returned value ARE null-prototype copies** (they are the ones that could one day carry a foreign datum). **The null-prototype choice is a `T` declaration and is reversible by a one-cell amendment; the DETACHMENT at depth is not reversible — it is `O-3`.**

**⟶ AMENDED `2026-10-11` (`UNIT-ADV-1`'s kick-back resolution, `SPEC-FIX`; `RCA-8(d)` ANNOTATE-BESIDE — the as-filed bytes above are KEPT VISIBLE and are not rewritten; the operative reading of the third sentence is stated here, and the record is `§9d` item 18). THE OPERATIVE READING: the NULL-PROTOTYPE clause binds every OBJECT the copy builds — `get()`'s record and any nested OBJECT — while an ARRAY copy stays a REAL array whose ELEMENTS are copied at depth. THE PRECEDENT THIS CONTRACT ITSELF CITES DECIDES IT, AT ITS OWN BYTES (`§0` ruling 6: *"THE LANDED CLONE PRECEDENT — THE SHAPE `O-3` ADOPTS"*, `L` `src/renderer/store-core-graph.ts:362-388`, the `G4-F5`/`G4-F6` amendment whose own doc-comment reads *"outputs are built with `Object.create(null)`, so a caller-owned hostile key rides as DATA, never as the copy's prototype"*): its `snapshotValue` answers `if (Array.isArray(candidate)) { const out: unknown[] = []; … return out }` — A REAL ARRAY — and answers `const out: Record<string, unknown> = Object.create(null)` for every OBJECT. So the precedent's discipline is exactly: OBJECTS null-prototype, ARRAYS real arrays. THE CONSEQUENCE, STATED: the third sentence's *"any array/object inside a returned value ARE null-prototype copies"* is read as the OBJECT half of that precedent; a null-prototype ARRAY has no `Array.prototype` — no iterator, no `.push`, no `.includes` — so it is unusable by any consumer, and it also falsifies `§2.5` item 3's own mutation-visibility control (which mutates the returned array). A row demanding a null-prototype ARRAY is therefore MALFORMED; a row demanding a null-prototype OBJECT (the record, or any nested object) is the operative reading and stands. THE FALSIFIERS THAT STILL REDDEN EACH HALF: a foreign prototype on the record or on a nested object; a null-prototype ARRAY copy (an array's prototype is `Array.prototype` by identity, and its `push`/`includes`/iterator must exist); a copy whose array ELEMENTS are not copied at depth (a shared element node); an undetached nested object. **A `SPEC-FIX` and not a `CONTRACT` change: no as-filed byte above moves, the clause's AUTHORITY (`T`, reversible by a one-cell amendment) is unchanged, and the red set's rows carry the reading (`tests/secure-store-discipline.test.ts` `P-O3-IM-2` attempt (7)).**
5. **THE READ-SURFACE CENSUS IS FROZEN AT THE MODULE'S OWN THREE VALUE-RETURNING MEMBERS, AND THE BRIDGE'S SUPERSET IS RECORDED BESIDE IT** (`T`, `CONTRACT`; `§2.5` item 3): `Object.keys(store)` is exactly **`["get","lastWriteReceipt","set"]`** — a fourth value-returning member of the returned object FAILS the census row. **The manual-UI channel's own carriers are RECORDED and NOT censused by this unit** (`L` `src/main/preload.ts`'s `security` member set and `L` `src/main/main.ts:384/394`'s `IPC_SECURITY_GET`/`IPC_SECURITY_SET` responses — the SET response's record is `{ ...settings, write }` and the GET response is `{ ...settings, exclusion }`): they cross a boundary that already copies, they belong to the channel, and **a carrier of theirs this unit never touches is not this unit's row to move** — while **a change to a carrier of theirs IS a forbidden diff** (`§5.1` item 3). **REVERSIBLE by a one-cell amendment if the architect rules the channel carriers into this unit's census.**
6. **NO RE-FREEZE, NO STORE-MODULE EDIT, NO UNION MOVE** (`T`, `NEGATIVE`; the wave's `P-7` discipline): this unit edits **`src/main/security-store.ts` alone** among production files; the frozen store module's bytes (`src/renderer/store-core-graph.ts` = `sha256:0664c52f…` · `src/renderer/store-graph-references.ts` = `sha256:5c0c1a97…`, the `HYDRATE-1` executed file pins), the store's 16-member refusal union, the `secure.*` refusal's single site, and every carrier named in `§5.1` are UNMOVED. **A diff that touches any of them is a COLLISION finding.** **Not reversible by declaration — this is the boundary of the admitted row.**

### 0B. The claim ledger — the closed authority enum and the closed claim-class set (the spec-writer gate rules this filing enforces)

1. **THE CLOSED AUTHORITY ENUM — every claim's authority is ONE of**: **`A`** (an architect ruling, by ruling id / row name / directive) · **`L`** (landed code, by `file:symbol`; a line reference is the `G3`-style landed-site citation and is a reading, not a contract) · **`M`** (a measured figure, with the measuring pass and its driver/commit named) · **`T`** (declared by THIS spec as a contract clause of this unit or as a working default). **A claim with no authority, or with an authority outside this enum, is a filing defect.** **A claim whose authority is an `INFERRED` form MUST carry, in the same cell, its reason and its evaluator, and its status is `unconfirmed` or `confirmed`** — this filing carries **ZERO `INFERRED` claims** (every claim below resolves onto `A`, `L`, `M` or `T`); a later pass that adds one MUST say which kind it is.
2. **THE CLOSED CLAIM-CLASS SET**: `CONTRACT` (what this unit must land) · `OBSERVATION` (today's state — falsifiable by the build) · `CENSUS` (a count, printed with its terms) · `CROSS-REF` (a citation) · `OWED` (a duty carried with an owner AND a positive revisit condition — never a bare `OWED`) · `DECLARED-DEFAULT` (a reversible working default with its alternative priced) · `NEGATIVE` (a finding class — what FAILS a row). **A claim whose class does not exist in this set is a filing defect.**
3. **THE SUPERSESSION POINTER EDGE** (binding on every annotation): a cell that supersedes or is superseded MUST carry a pointer to the live form (a `§`/row id or a path) — a superseded cell with no successor pointer is a dead end and a finding. **This filing supersedes nothing by edit**; it names the two cells whose EFFECT it supersedes-or-amends (`docs/specs/store-security.md` `§2.1` item 2, already pointer-carrying, and `§2.3` item 5, whose pointer this unit's landing adds — `§5.1` item 2) and carries the **successor of both: this file.**
4. **THE PARAMETER-SEMANTICS RULE**: every declared parameter of this unit's declared surfaces has a row spelling its **unit**, its **domain**, its **referent** and its **evaluator**, and the values **OUTSIDE** the domain with the declared answer for each; a declared parameter without a row is a gate-2 finding. **The table is `§6`.**
5. **THE STATUS-LINE RULE**: the status line at the head of this file is the ONE authoritative status statement (filing state · unit · wave · ledger row · gate-1 state); no later section may contradict it without an annotate-beside dated note.

## 1. Scope

### 1.1 What the unit is

1. **Three repairs in one landed artifact** (`A`/`T`, `CONTRACT`): **(a) `O-1`** — the record's advance is ordered **AFTER a successful save**, and a refusal returns the **PRE-WRITE** record, so **live state equals durable state** (`§2.1` items 2/3, `§2.4`); **(b) `O-2`** — the write path carries a **VALUE-PRESERVATION ADMISSION** whose refusal is whole-patch, with the documented `sanitize()` coercions preserved inside its boundary (`§2.2`); **(c) `O-3`** — the read surface's value-returning members return **DEEP, cycle-safe, prototype-safe** copies, `lastWriteReceipt()` included (`§2.5`).
2. **The single writer, unmoved** (`T`/`L`, `CROSS-REF`): `src/main/security-store.ts`'s persist is the ONLY writer of `provident-security.json`'s bytes; the persisted set stays EXACTLY the two files `{provident-security.json, provident-settings.json}` (the `G2` DONE pin). **This unit adds no file, no path, no directory and no writer.**
3. **The tier's reach, unmoved** (`A`/`T`, `CROSS-REF`): the tier is reachable ONLY from `main`'s boot gate construction, the two manual-UI IPC handlers, and the pane's own snapshot read; **this unit adds no reach, no name-addressing and no MCP route** (`O-2`'s admission is a check on a VALUE, never a name mechanism — name-addressing is `S2`'s and stays unopened, `§5.1`).
4. **The layer** (`T`, `CONTRACT`): `[H]` for the write path's ordering and the admission's placement (the module is main-process code); `[T]` for every row driven in-process against the module (the red set's own harness) and `[H]`/`[T]` for any row that boots a scratch realm. **No timing figure is claimed anywhere in this file.**
5. **Gate 1's discharge, stated as the unit's permission to run** (`A`, `CROSS-REF`; `§0` ruling 5): these clauses carry no outstanding `must-fix` question; what this unit needs from the architect is **the spec gate's approval alone** (`AGENTS.md` item 10a — the one approval the chain waits for), and after that the chain runs on the orchestrator's authority through red → green → gates 4–10.

### 1.4 The layer honesty

1. Every behavioural claim here is **main-process module evidence** — `[H]` for the write path's ordering and the admission's placement, `[T]` for the rows a node-process harness drives against the module. **The rows that boot a real realm are `[H]`/`[U]`-adjacent and are NOT claimed by this unit's red set** (the live arms of the measured Family E are cited as `M`, never re-authored here; `§5.2` item 4).
2. **No timing figure is claimed** (the family's `RCA-12` discipline): the write-order rows assert ORDER and OUTCOME, never duration; the copy rows assert IDENTITY and DETACHMENT, never cost.
3. **This green is main-process/module evidence, never an APP claim.** It proves the record's advance order, the admission's boundary, the refusal's consequences and the read surface's detachment against the module: it does **not** prove the assembled app's rendered behaviour, the pane's pixels, the MCP gate's semantics, or the fork's own carrier's behaviour. **A green here is not an APP-green, and no row below may be reported as one** (`§5.6.2` item 1, `§7` item 1).
4. The full `npm test` context is whatever the supervisor measures at this unit's gate 9; this unit's red set is authored **against this spec alone** (`§4.1`), in this unit's own test file.

## 2. The surface (exact)

### 2.1 The module's declared surface — `src/main/security-store.ts` (the principal and ONLY production edit target)

1. **THE EXPORT CENSUS IS UNMOVED IN SHAPE** (`CENSUS`, `L`): the module exports `createSecurityStore` (a function), and the types `SecurityStoreOptions` · `SecurityStore` · `SecurityWriteReceipt`. **`get()`, `set()`, `lastWriteReceipt()` are members of the object the factory returns**, not module exports (`L` `:117-145`). **THIS UNIT ADDS NO EXPORTED NAME** — the admission predicate is module-internal (`T`, `§2.2` item 1), **and a diff that exports a new name, or that renames or removes one of the four, is a FAILING static row** (`§5.6.1` `P-O3-TP-1`'s attempt (2)(b)), because the module's public shape is the tier's whole contract face.
2. **`createSecurityStore(opts: SecurityStoreOptions): SecurityStore` — LANDED, signature and boot posture UNCHANGED** (`L` `:51-61`, `T`, `CONTRACT`): `SecurityStoreOptions = { path: string }`. **THE BOOT READ'S FAIL-STATES ARE UNCHANGED:** a **missing** file → the first-run default `{ token: null, enabled: ['read','dispatch'], maxJournalLength: undefined }`; a **corrupt** file (unparsable bytes) → the SAME default; a path that **IS a directory** → `readFileSync` throws `EISDIR`, the catch answers the SAME default (LIVE-measured, `SC-E-05`) — **NEVER a throw, in any of the three cases.** **The constructor writes NO file** (the boot read is a read; no default is persisted until the first successful `set()`) — **stated because `O-1`'s "live equals durable" is a claim about a record whose bytes EXIST** (`§2.3` item 7(i)/(ii)).
3. **THE WRITE PATH'S ORDER, PINNED IN SIX STEPS** (`T`, `CONTRACT`; `§0A` items 2/3; reading (b)). **`set(patch)` is total over its declared domain, never throws, and performs these steps IN ORDER** (a step 0 stands UNSTATED in this sequence and is documented at `CURRENT STATE` item 3 and `§2.1` item 4: the parent directory is CREATED by `mkdirSync(dirname, {recursive:true})` INSIDE `persist()`, which is why a nonexistent parent is not one of the refusal arms — see the note beside `§6` `PAR-1`'s OUTSIDE column):
   1. **ADMISSION** — the representability check of `§2.2` runs over the patch's declared members. **A patch containing any unrepresentable value is REFUSED HERE, whole-patch:** the live record is untouched, no filesystem call is made, the receipt is `{status:'refused', reason:'write-failed'}`, and `set()` returns the **PRE-WRITE** record.
   2. **COERCION / CANDIDATE** — the admitted patch is coerced by the landed `sanitize()` rules into a **CANDIDATE** record (`§2.2` item 3). **The candidate is a LOCAL of the call: `current` is NOT assigned here.** Its members are `token: string | null` · `enabled: string[]` (the five-`VALID_GROUPS`-filtered, deduplicated, order-preserving set) · `maxJournalLength: number | undefined`.
   3. **STAGING + PERSIST** — the candidate is handed to `persist()`, which stages it to `${path}.tmp`, `fsync`s the tmp file, and `renameSync`s it onto the real path. **`persist()` reads NO closure state: it writes the CANDIDATE it is given** (`§0A` item 3).
   4. **THE COMMIT POINT — the successful `renameSync`.** **The record advances (`current = candidate`) HERE and ONLY here** — at the first instant the file's bytes ARE the candidate. **A failure BEFORE this point is a refusal and the record does not move; there is no third possibility** (`§0A` item 2).
   5. **THE DURABILITY STRENGTHENING** — the parent directory is `fsync`'ed. **⟶ THIRD NOTE BESIDE THIS STEP, DATED `2026-10-11` (THE POST-DONE AMENDMENT PASS; `A`/`T`; `RCA-8(d)`: the as-filed step and BOTH notes below stand — this is a pointer, not a rewrite).** **`§2.6` (THE WRITE LOCK) GOVERNS WHERE THIS STEP'S OPERATIVE READING AND `§0A` ITEM 2 DIFFER:** the record's advance MUST land at the commit (the successful `renameSync`) — so a read taken at THIS step's own `fsync` must already answer the CANDIDATE, and this step's *"NOT SEPARATELY OBSERVABLE"* claim (see the `PBT-10` note below) is QUALIFIED IN EFFECT, while its ORDER+OUTCOME half stands. **A failure of THIS step is still NOT a refusal and moves nothing back — that half is unchanged and is not qualified by `§2.6`** (`§2.6` item 5's precedence clauses (i)/(ii)/(iii)). **A failure here is NOT a refusal: the write stays `committed`, the record stays advanced, and live still equals durable** (`§0A` item 2; the rationale is stated there, and the `G3` `NEW-2` re-aim is the precedent). **OPERATIVE READING (`PBT-10`, GATE 4, disposition `SPEC-FIX`; dated `2026-10-11`, annotated BESIDE the cell — the as-filed bytes above stand, `RCA-8(d)`):** this step's position is the ORDER of the five substeps INSIDE `persist()` (item 4) — and **the instant "the record advances BEFORE this step" is NOT SEPARATELY OBSERVABLE through this unit's declared surface**, because `persist()` is called BY `set()` and returns only after the directory `fsync` has succeeded or failed (`L` `src/main/security-store.ts:198-236`): by the time `set()` can advance the record, `persist()` has already returned, so no caller-visible instant sits between the rename and the directory `fsync`. **This item therefore declares ORDER and OUTCOME only** — the record advances only after a successful rename, and a failure of THIS step is not a refusal — and **it does NOT claim an observable intermediate instant.** What the declared surface DOES pin is the same claim from the other side, and that is how the register drives it: at the PRE-rename tmp-fsync probe the staged file already carries the candidate while the real path's bytes are still the pre-write record (`§5.6.1` `P-M-SM-1` attempt (b)), and at the post-rename instant the real path's bytes ARE the candidate with the record advanced (`P-M-SM-1` attempt (d)) — **live equals durable at every instant the module's own surface can be read.** **⟶ SECOND OPERATIVE READING BESIDE THIS STEP, DATED `2026-10-11` (GATES 7+8, from the gate-5 finding `docs/specs/secure-store-discipline-greens.md` `§6` finding 2; `RCA-8(d)`: the as-filed step and the `PBT-10` note above BOTH stand — this note is the third clause, not a rewrite).** **THE `PBT-10` NOTE'S OBSERVABILITY SENTENCE IS REFUTED AT THIS UNIT'S OWN SURFACE:** *"the instant 'the record advances BEFORE this step' is NOT SEPARATELY OBSERVABLE through this unit's declared surface"* is FALSE — a RE-ENTRANT `get()` called from inside the parent-directory `fsync` DOES observe the instant, because this module's `persist()` performs that `fsync` INSIDE itself and `set()` advances the record only AFTER `persist()` RETURNS (`src/main/security-store.ts:293` then `:297`). **MEASURED (greens `SSD-G-81`):** at that instant the re-entrant `get()` answers the PRE-WRITE record (`{token:'PRE', maxJournalLength:100}`) while the real path's bytes ALREADY carry the candidate (`{token:'DIRFSYNC', maxJournalLength:42}`), and the receipt is `{"status":"committed"}` with the record advanced by the time `set()` returns. **WHAT STILL HOLDS, AND WHY NO ROW MOVES:** this step's OUTCOME half (a failure here is NOT a refusal; the write stays `committed`; the record stays advanced) is exactly what `§2.4` `R-5` and the greens' `SSD-G-09` drive, and `§3.3` `I-1` is phrased *"after every attempt"* — so **no declared row expectation is violated and no row is re-grained.** **THE RESIDUAL IS THE ARCHITECT'S (OWED; the contract's `§9e`, OWED item (c)):** the answer's own order sentence (*"ordered after the successful `renameSync` … and BEFORE the parent-directory `fsyncSync`"*) is NOT what the landed bytes do — the advance is AFTER `persist()` returns, i.e. after the directory `fsync` has been attempted — so either the sentence is amended to the landed order (a one-cell amendment, priced at `§0A` item 2's own reversibility clause) or the module is re-ordered to make the sentence literally true (a `src/**` change outside this pass's wall). **The alternative reading (an observable advance-before-the-dir-fsync instant) is NOT available from this contract's declared surface and is not asserted by any row.**
   6. **THE ANSWER** — `set()` returns `this.get()`, i.e. the record now live: **the candidate's record on a committed write, the PRE-WRITE record on a refusal.** `lastWriteReceipt()` answers the receipt of THIS attempt (never null after an attempt).
4. **`persist()`'s declared shape after this unit** (`T`, `CONTRACT`; `L`'s landed five-step body kept): a **pure-in-its-inputs** closure that takes the candidate record, returns `SecurityWriteReceipt`, and **touches no record state**. **⟶ ANNOTATED BESIDE, DATED `2026-10-11` (THE POST-DONE AMENDMENT PASS; `RCA-8(d)`: the as-filed sentence above stands and is not rewritten).** **THE *"touches no record state"* CLAUSE IS READ AS *"READS NO CLOSURE STATE AND TAKES THE CANDIDATE AS ITS INPUT"* (`§0A` item 3) — the reading `§2.6` item 5's mechanism `(h-i)` preserves:** if the commit-landing sink lands, it is invoked BY `persist()` at the attempt's terminal and it touches the **CALLER's** closure, never `persist()`'s own inputs, so `persist()` still reads no closure state and is still never given the closure's `current`. **A `persist()` that reads `current`, or that advances the record on its own authority, remains OUTSIDE this shape and FAILS `P-M-SM-2`'s attempt (1)** (`§6` `PAR-7` carries the matching dated note). **Its fail-states: a failed tmp write · a failed tmp `fsync` · a failed `renameSync`** each answer `{status:'refused', reason:'write-failed'}`; **a successful rename answers `{status:'committed'}` regardless of the directory `fsync`'s outcome** (`§0A` item 2). **Its tmp discipline is UNCHANGED** (`L` `:97-114`): the tmp path is **never parsed as the record**; a stale tmp is overwritten by the next write; a caught failure removes its own tmp **best-effort, with its own failure swallowed** (the `F-11` host fix, unmoved). **The one addition: an ADMISSION refusal never reaches `persist()` at all** (`§2.1` item 3's step 1).
5. **`get(): SecuritySettings` — the DEEP detached record of the live state** (`T`, `CONTRACT`; `§2.5` items 1/2): a fresh copy of `current`, **detached at every depth, cycle-safe, prototype-safe** (`Object.create(null)` + a `seen` map), whose `enabled` array is a fresh array. **The members are exactly `token` · `enabled` · `maxJournalLength`** — no member is added and none is renamed (`L` `src/shared/types.ts:316-323` is the referent type; an optional member may be PRESENT with the value `undefined`, which is the landed shape's own spelling — a consumer treats absent and `undefined` alike).
6. **`lastWriteReceipt(): SecurityWriteReceipt | null` — a FRESH copy per call** (`T`, `CONTRACT`; `§2.5` item 3): `null` **ONLY before the first `set()` attempt** through this store instance. After **every** attempt — committed, admission-refused or persist-refused — it answers a **detached copy** of that attempt's receipt: **the tier's own receipt object is never handed out, and two calls never answer the same object.** The copy is a plain object literal carrying `status` (and `reason` on a refusal) — **the same shape as `SecurityWriteReceipt`**, so a consumer typed to `{ status: 'committed' } | { status: 'refused'; reason: 'write-failed' }` reads it identically.
7. **`SecurityWriteReceipt` — the closed form, UNMOVED** (`T`/`A`, `CONTRACT`; `§0` ruling 7): `{ status: 'committed' } | { status: 'refused'; reason: 'write-failed' }`. **EXACTLY ONE of the two answers after every attempt. There is NO third status, NO third form, NO second reason token, NO `dropped` member, and NO `null` reason.** **`SecurityWriteReceipt` is NOT a member of the store's 16-member refusal union and MUST NOT BECOME one** (`A`: the `D-GATE` row's clause (2), the channel-token precedent).

### 2.2 `O-2` — THE JSON-SAFETY ADMISSION, derived exactly

1. **THE PREDICATE IS A DECLARED, CLOSED, VALUE-PRESERVING RULE — and it is NOT a serialization** (`T`, `CONTRACT`; `A` answer (2): *"a value-preservation test on the write path, never a serialization"*). **Declared name (module-internal, never exported): `representableValue(v: unknown): boolean`.** **A value `v` is REPRESENTABLE iff ALL of the following hold:** **(i)** `v` is one of the four JSON primitive kinds reachable without conversion — `null` · a `boolean` · a **finite `number`** · a `string`; **OR** **(ii)** `v` is an **array or an ordinary object** whose **own enumerable string-keyed members** are each themselves representable, **with NO path revisiting a node it already visited** (the **cycle guard**: an alias encountered twice on one walk is NOT representable), **AND** `typeof v !== 'function'`. **`Symbol`s, `BigInt`s, functions, `Map`, `Set`, `Date` and any other non-plain object are NOT representable (`(i)` fails and `(ii)` fails); `NaN`, `Infinity` and `-Infinity` are NOT representable (`(i)` fails — "finite"); a cyclic structure is NOT representable (the cycle guard); a value carrying an own enumerable `__proto__` **key** is representable (it is an own data property, not a prototype assignment).** **The predicate is TOTAL: it returns a `boolean` for EVERY input and NEVER throws** (`Symbol.iterator`-less values, hostile proxies included — a host object that throws on property access is caught and answers NOT representable).
2. **WHAT THE PREDICATE MAY BE EQUIVALENT TO, AND WHAT IT MAY NOT BE IMPLEMENTED AS** (`T`, `CONTRACT` + `NEGATIVE`): a TestWriter may assert the predicate through the **observable** it defines — **the admitted-and-persisted value equals the value the caller supplied, for the members whose declared form IS the caller's value** — and the red set MAY drive the equivalence `representableValue(v) === (v survives a JSON round trip AND preserves every member)` as a CONTROL. **A JSON round-trip MUST NOT be the implementation of the check as the write path's admission gate**: a round-trip THROWS on cycles and `BigInt`, and the write path is TOTAL (`§2.3` item 1). **A red row that observes the module throwing on a cyclic patch FAILS.**
3. **THE BOUNDARY — WHERE THE DOCUMENTED `sanitize()` COERCION ENDS AND REFUSAL BEGINS** (`T`, `CONTRACT`; THE DERIVED RULE, stated so a test can drive it). **The landed, documented coercions STAY, inside this exact boundary:** for `token` — **a non-empty `string` is the value; `''` and `null` CLEAR it** (`null`); for `maxJournalLength` — **a finite positive `number` is `Math.floor`-ed** (so `50.7` → `50`, and a positive number below `1` floors to `0`, which **is** the declared clear), **`0`, `-0`, any negative number and `null` CLEAR it** (`undefined`); for `groups`/`disable` — **an ARRAY is filtered through the five `VALID_GROUPS` (`read` · `dispatch` · `graph` · `code` · `module`), deduplicated, with current-state order preserved; non-group strings are dropped** (the landed, documented filter). **THE RULE, in one sentence: an unrepresentable value is admissible ONLY as a DECLARED CLEAR of its own member** — i.e. `null` for `token` / `maxJournalLength` and **nothing else** — **and every other unrepresentable value REFUSES THE WHOLE PATCH.** So: `undefined` as a member value is the **absent** marker (the landed semantics: the member keeps its current value — **the write is admitted**); `null` on `token`/`maxJournalLength` is a DECLARED CLEAR (**admitted**); `0`/negatives/`NaN` on `maxJournalLength` are **declared-domain values the sanitize documents as "else cleared"** (**admitted, and they clear** — note `NaN` is unrepresentable but sits in the documented clear arm, which is exactly why it needs this rule rather than the predicate alone); **`Infinity`/`-Infinity`/`BigInt`/a `function`/a `Symbol`/`Map`/`Set`/`Date`/a cyclic object — on ANY declared member, and at ANY depth inside an array — REFUSE the patch.**
4. **WHY THE PREDICATE GOVERNS A WHOLE MEMBER AND NOT MERELY AN ELEMENT** (`T`, `CONTRACT`; the derived consequence, stated because it MOVES a landed behaviour): the landed filter silently DROPS a non-group element from `groups`/`disable` **and answers `committed`** — which is `SECURITY-STORE-COMMITTED-RECEIPT-ON-A-DROPPED-VALUE`'s exact subject. **After this unit: a non-STRING element inside the array is still dropped by the documented filter (admitted); a non-REPRESENTABLE element (`BigInt(7)`, a function, a nested cycle) REFUSES THE WHOLE PATCH** — no `committed` receipt ever answers a value the write discarded. **The distinction is the two axes meeting: the COERCION axis is a declared filter over an admitted domain; the ADMISSION axis is a refusal of a value the tier cannot carry. Where the two meet, §2.2 item 3's rule decides, and this item's example is the derived consequence.**
5. **THE REFUSAL'S TERMS ARE EXACT** (`T`, `CONTRACT`): a refusal at admission is **(a)** whole-patch — **no partial write, no member applied "up to" the bad one**; **(b)** accompanied by the landed refused receipt, never a throw; **(c)** a **no-op on the filesystem** — no `${path}.tmp` is created, no existing file is touched, the real path's record is byte-identical before and after; **(d)** a **no-op on the record** — the LIVE record equals the pre-attempt record member for member; **(e)** answered by `get()` as the PRE-WRITE record.
6. **THE ADMISSION'S SCOPE IS THE DECLARED PATCH AND NOTHING ELSE** (`T`, `NEGATIVE`): the check runs over the **patch object's declared members** (`token`, `groups`, `disable`, `maxJournalLength`). **A patch member OUTSIDE the declared set is not this unit's subject** — the landed posture (an unknown key never reaches the record; the class is filed as `SECURITY-STORE-SILENT-KEY-DROP`, owned by `S2`) is **UNCHANGED, and this unit neither fixes, moves nor claims it** (this item's own scope clause; the class is `S2`'s — `§1.3` item 1). **The record itself is never admitted** — it is the module's own three-member value and is representable by construction.

### 2.3 `O-1` — THE ROLLBACK, and what a refusal leaves behind

1. **THE NEVER-THROWS RULE, RESTATED AS THIS UNIT'S FIRST PROPERTY** (`T`, `CONTRACT`): **`set()` and every other declared member are TOTAL over their declared domains: no patch shape, no filesystem state and no hostile value makes a declared member throw.** The landed exception — `set(null)` throwing a `TypeError` on the member read (`M`, `SC-E-01`'s recorded out-of-domain observation) — **is CLOSED here rather than left as a documented wart**: a non-object patch (including `null`/`undefined`/a primitive/a function) is answered as a **whole-patch refusal** (the receipt of `§2.1` item 7, the record unmoved) and **never throws**. **This is a declared behaviour change with its own red row, and it is required by `O-2`'s own answer (the value cannot be persisted as JSON) plus the totality the receipt's design rests on.**
2. **THE RECORD-ADVANCE INVARIANT** (`T`, `CONTRACT`; `§2.1` item 3's steps 1–5): **the live record advances IFF a save succeeded** — the assignment happens at the commit point and nowhere else. **An implementation that assigns first and rolls back afterwards is FAILING**, even if its observable answers match on the paths a given test drives: the invariant is about the RECORD's state at every instant, and the measured defect (`:138-139`) is exactly that shape. **The red set MAY assert this both ways (the ordered form and the observable form), and a row that only checks the observable form MUST say so.**
3. **WHAT HAPPENS TO THE PREVIOUSLY-PERSISTED FILE ON A REFUSAL** (`T`, `CONTRACT`; `L`'s landed rule kept): the real path's record is **byte-identical** to the pre-write record — for **all four** refusal classes: **(a) admission** (no filesystem call at all), **(b) a failed tmp write**, **(c) a failed tmp `fsync`**, **(d) a failed `renameSync`**. **A TORN FILE AT THE REAL PATH IS IMPOSSIBLE** — the rename is atomic on one filesystem, and the record at the real path is only ever one of the two well-formed records.
4. **THE RESIDUAL `${path}.tmp` ON A REFUSED PATH** (`T`, `CONTRACT`; `L` `:97-114` kept): **(a)** a SUCCESSFUL persist leaves **NO** `${path}.tmp` (the rename consumed it); **(b)** on a CAUGHT failure the staging tmp is removed **best-effort, with its own removal failure SWALLOWED** — a stale tmp that happens to name a DIRECTORY (or a protected path) **must not make `set()` throw** (the landing's `F-11` host fix, re-asserted as this unit's property); **(c)** a stale tmp is **never parsed as the record** and is overwritten by the next write; **(d)** an **ADMISSION** refusal leaves **no new tmp** because it wrote none. **A residual tmp is a tolerated, documented residue; a THROW from the cleanup is FAILING.**
5. **THE EXACT POST-REFUSAL `get()` ANSWER** (`T`, `CONTRACT`): **the PRE-WRITE record, member for member** — same `token` (including `null`), the same `enabled` sequence in the same order, the same `maxJournalLength` (including its absent/`undefined` spelling). **A `get()` that answers any member of the refused patch is FAILING.**
6. **THE RECEIPT'S `status`/`reason` FOR A FAILED SAVE** (`T`, `CONTRACT`; `§2.1` item 7): **`{ status: 'refused', reason: 'write-failed' }`** — the same token at all three filesystem failure points AND at the admission refusal (`§0A` item 1). **On a committed write, `{ status: 'committed' }` and NO `reason` member.** **A refusal whose `reason` differs, is absent, is `undefined` or is a second spelling FAILS.**
7. **WHETHER ANY IN-MEMORY-ONLY STATE MAY EXIST AT ALL** (`T`, `NEGATIVE`; reading (b), clause (1)): **NO.** After a successful write the live record and the file's bytes agree (the post-commit directory `fsync` is a durability strengthening, not a content divergence — `§0A` item 2); **a live record that carries a value the file does not hold IS THE DEFECT THIS UNIT REPAIRS.** **Two declared, non-divergent states are recorded so the rule is not over-read:** **(i) THE PRE-FIRST-WRITE STATE** — a cold store at a path with no file holds the first-run default while no file exists; **this is not a divergence but the absence of a durable state, and the first successful write creates the file** (the `G2` `VERSION-FROM-FIRST-WRITE` shape); **(ii) THE BOOT-FALLBACK STATE** — a corrupt/unreadable file leaves the record at the defaults while the unreadable bytes remain at the path; **the record is then the file's declared reader-answer, and the NEXT successful write replaces the bytes with the record** — the two agree from that point on. **A third state — a record advanced past a refusal — does not exist after this unit.**

### 2.4 The refusal semantics in one table (state · trigger · required behaviour) — each row also a `M-*`/`F-*` subject at `§3`

**Every row is `T`/`CONTRACT` unless a cell cites otherwise; the trigger is exact, and each row names the register row that drives it (`§5.6.1`).**

| # | State | Trigger (exact) | Required behaviour | Driven by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **R-1** | **ADMISSION REFUSAL** | a patch carrying an unrepresentable value on `token`, `groups`/`disable` (as an element **or** as the array itself), or `maxJournalLength` (and the non-object patch of `§2.3` item 1) | whole-patch refusal; **no filesystem call**; the real path's record byte-identical; `get()` = the pre-write record; receipt `{status:'refused', reason:'write-failed'}` | `P-O2-IM-1`, `P-O1-TP-1`, `P-M-SM-1` (e) |
| **R-2** | **TMP-WRITE REFUSAL** | the staging write fails (parent is a regular FILE · parent directory `0500` · tmp path unavailable) | previous file intact and byte-identical; a stale tmp removed best-effort with its own failure swallowed; record unmoved; `get()` = pre-write; refused receipt | `P-O1-TP-1`, `P-O2-IM-2` |
| **R-3** | **TMP-`fsync` REFUSAL** | the tmp file's `fsyncSync` fails (pre-rename) | previous file intact; record unmoved; `get()` = pre-write; refused receipt; no throw | `P-O1-TP-1` |
| **R-4** | **RENAME REFUSAL** | `renameSync(tmp, path)` fails (e.g. the target path IS a DIRECTORY) | previous file intact; a residual tmp is possible and is overwritten next and never parsed; record unmoved; `get()` = pre-write; refused receipt; **no throw** | `P-O1-TP-1`, `P-O2-IM-2` |
| **R-5** | **POST-COMMIT DIRECTORY-`fsync` FAILURE** | the rename SUCCEEDED and the parent directory's `fsyncSync` fails | **`{status:'committed'}`, the record ADVANCED, live equals durable**; **NOT a refusal and NOT a rollback** (`§0A` item 2) | `P-O1-TP-1` (class 5), `P-M-SM-1` (f) |
| **R-6** | **COMMITTED** | every step succeeded | the file's bytes are the candidate; the record advanced at the rename; `get()` = the candidate's record; `{status:'committed'}`; **NO tmp remains** | `P-O1-TP-1`, `P-M-SM-1` (d) |
| **R-7** | **READ-LOCK WINDOW** *(APPENDED `2026-10-11`, the post-DONE amendment; `§2.6`; `R-1`…`R-6` keep their numbers and their cells)* | **a write IS IN FLIGHT** — its window is OPEN (from its first filesystem effect to its terminal: the commit landing or a refusal terminal) — and a **value-returning member is read from inside that window** (a re-entrant read: from inside the attempt's own fs call, from a hook it invokes, or from the write path itself) | **THE LOCK: the read surface is served from the DURABLE state — `get()` answers the record the real path's bytes carry at that instant (the PRE-WRITE record before the commit lands; the CANDIDATE at and after it); `lastWriteReceipt()` answers the last LANDED attempt's closed form, or the declared pre-first-landing `null` (the in-flight attempt's own receipt is WITHHELD until its terminal); the pair flips AT the terminal; nothing throws, blocks or waits.** An ADMISSION refusal opens no window at all | `P-M-SM-3`, `M-8`, `F-13`, `I-10` |

### 2.5 `O-3` — THE FULL-SURFACE DEEP CLONE

1. **THE COPY'S DISCIPLINE** (`T`, `CONTRACT`; `§0` ruling 6, `§0A` item 4): **every value a declared member returns is a DETACHED COPY, at every depth** — built with **`Object.create(null)`** as the prototype and a **`seen` map** as the cycle guard; **no aliasing at any depth** (**a holder's mutation of any returned sub-value is invisible to the next read, and to the tier's own state**). **A JSON round-trip is INADMISSIBLE** as the implementation (`§2.2` item 2's reason, and it would throw where this discipline must not).
2. **`get()`'s return is an instance of that discipline** (`T`, `CONTRACT`): the record object **and** its `enabled` array are fresh; the two values are primitives. **Depth-completeness is asserted STRUCTURALLY** (the walk returns no alias of any node it copied) rather than by observing only today's three-member shape — so a later record member that is itself an object is covered by construction.
3. **THE CLOSED READ-SURFACE SET — EVERY MEMBER NAMED** (`T`, `CENSUS`; `§0A` item 5): **`Object.keys(createSecurityStore({path}))` is EXACTLY `["get","lastWriteReceipt","set"]`** — in the landed order, and the census is a TERM (**a fourth value-returning member FAILS; a member REMOVED fails too**). **The three members, and what each returns:** **`get()`** → the deep detached `SecuritySettings` record (`§2.1` item 5) · **`set(patch)`** → `this.get()`, i.e. the same deep detached record (the pre-write one on a refusal) · **`lastWriteReceipt()`** → a fresh detached `SecurityWriteReceipt` copy, or `null` before the first attempt (`§2.1` item 6). **The channel's carriers are outside this census and unedited** (`§0A` item 5).
4. **`lastWriteReceipt()`'s SHAPE AND DETACHMENT, PINNED** (`T`, `CONTRACT`): **(a) the shape** is `{ status: 'committed' }` or `{ status: 'refused', reason: 'write-failed' }` or `null` (pre-first-attempt only) — **no other member, no `undefined` `reason` on a refusal**; **(b) the detachment**: two successive calls **never answer the same object** (`a === b` is `false`), and **a holder's mutation of the returned object is INVISIBLE to the tier and to the next call** — the mutated copy must not be able to rewrite the tier's answer to its last write (the measured `SC-E-07` defect, closed). **THE TIER'S OWN RECEIPT OBJECT IS NEVER HANDED OUT.**
5. **THE CENSUS'S POSITIVE CONTROL** (`T`, `NEGATIVE`-class control, `CENSUS`): the detachment detector is **NOT vacuous** — driven against a subject that hands out ONE shared object, it **fires**; against a subject that returns a fresh copy, it **does not**. **The control is a term of the register row that carries it** (`§5.6.1` `P-O3-IM-2`'s attempt (8)), because a detector that cannot fail proves nothing.

### 2.6 THE WRITE LOCK — READS ARE LOCKED OUT UNTIL THE COMMIT LANDS (the architect's answer to this unit's residual intra-write ORDER question, `A`: *"Lock out the reads until the commit lands"*; every clause below is `T`, `CONTRACT`, DECLARED by this amendment, and the witness is the gate-5 blind row `SSD-G-81`, `M`)

**⟶ THIS SUBSECTION IS AN INSERTION, DATED `2026-10-11` (THE POST-DONE AMENDMENT PASS — the pass that follows the gate-10 close-out and its commit `54f6688`; `RCA-8(d)`: NO as-filed byte of `§0`…`§9e` is rewritten, `§2.1`–`§2.5` keep their numbers AND their items, this is `§2.6` APPENDED, and every note this amendment adds elsewhere sits DATED beside the cell it reads — `§0A` item 2, `§2.1` item 3 step 5 and item 4, `§6` `PAR-1`/`PAR-7`/`PAR-10`, `§7c`/`§7d`, `§9e`.)**

1. **THE CLAUSE, IN ONE SENTENCE** (`A`, `CONTRACT`). **WHILE A WRITE IS IN FLIGHT, READS ARE LOCKED OUT UNTIL THE COMMIT LANDS: no value-returning member may be answered from the IN-FLIGHT attempt's un-landed state — neither the PRE-WRITE record once the commit has landed, nor the uncommitted CANDIDATE before it does.** The read surface is served from the DURABLE state at every instant of the write: the in-flight candidate is a **LOCAL** of the call (`§2.1` item 3 step 2) until the commit lands, and the in-flight attempt's own receipt is **WITHHELD** until its terminal lands (item 3(b)).

2. **THE WINDOW, PINNED AT BOTH ENDS** (`T`, `CONTRACT`). The lock window **OPENS at the attempt's FIRST FILESYSTEM EFFECT** — the first `mkdirSync`/`writeFileSync` the attempt performs — and **CLOSES at the attempt's TERMINAL**, which is exactly one of two things: **(i) THE COMMIT LANDING — the successful `renameSync`** (`§2.1` item 3 step 4; `§0A` item 2), the first instant the real path's bytes ARE the candidate; or **(ii) THE REFUSAL TERMINAL** — the caught failure at one of the three pre-rename points (the tmp write, the tmp `fsync`, the `renameSync`). **An ADMISSION refusal** (`§2.2` item 5(c)) **touches no filesystem and therefore NEVER OPENS A WINDOW**: a read taken across such an attempt is an ordinary pre-window read, and a row expecting a lock reading from it is **MALFORMED**. **The window is an ORDERED interval of the attempt's own steps, NOT a duration: no timing figure is claimed anywhere** (`§1.4` item 2).

3. **WHAT EACH DECLARED MEMBER ANSWERS INSIDE THE WINDOW — EXHAUSTIVE OVER THE FROZEN CENSUS** (`T`, `CONTRACT`). The census is EXACTLY the three members (`§2.5` item 3; `§6` `PAR-11`), **so these three are all of it; the lock creates no fourth member and no lock-only spelling.**
   **(a) `get()` — THE DURABLE RECORD AT THAT INSTANT.** Inside the window `get()` answers a `SecuritySettings` carrying EXACTLY the three declared members (`§6` `PAR-11` binds — no member added, renamed or made absent by the lock): **before the commit lands, the PRE-WRITE record, which is what the real path's bytes carry at that instant; at and after the commit landing, the CANDIDATE, which is what the real path's bytes carry from that instant.** **WHY THIS READING AND NOT "NO ANSWER", DERIVED SO IT IS NOT READ AS A CONVENIENCE** (`T`): a declared member must ANSWER (`§2.3` item 1 — total over its domain, *"the answer is a value … and never an exception"*), and its answer must carry exactly the three declared members (`§6` `PAR-11`); **so the two durable records are the ONLY admissible answers and the pairing in item 4 is FORCED by those two clauses rather than chosen here.**
   **(b) `lastWriteReceipt()` — THE LAST *LANDED* ATTEMPT'S CLOSED FORM; THE IN-FLIGHT ATTEMPT IS WITHHELD.** Inside the window the member answers the receipt of the most recent attempt **whose TERMINAL has landed** — that attempt's closed form — or **`null` while NO attempt has landed through this instance yet**, which includes the window of the instance's FIRST attempt (there the store has still *"answered nothing about a write"*, `§6` `PAR-10`'s own referent; the dated note beside `PAR-10` records this ONE declared in-window case). **The in-flight attempt's own receipt is NOT answerable inside its own window; it becomes answerable at its terminal, and for a committing write its form at that instant is `{status:'committed'}`** (`§0A` item 2 — the write IS committed from the rename onward and the directory `fsync` cannot undo it). **NO NEW TOKEN, NO NEW FORM, NO UNION MEMBER:** the member still answers EXACTLY one of the closed two forms or the declared pre-first-landing `null` (`§2.1` items 6/7; `§6` `PAR-8`/`PAR-9`/`PAR-10`); `'write-failed'` stays absent from the store's `16`-member refusal union and the receipt stays a **CHANNEL** vocabulary (`§2.1` item 7; the `D-GATE` row's clause (2)).
   **(c) `set()` — `this.get()`, its shape UNCHANGED** (`§2.1` item 3 step 6): the candidate's record on a committed write, the **PRE-WRITE** record on a refusal. `set()` can only return AFTER its own attempt's terminal, so **its return is always outside the window** and this clause does not move it.
   **(d) ANY OTHER VALUE-RETURNING MEMBER — THERE IS NONE, AND THE LOCK ADDS NONE** (`§2.5` item 3; `§3.2` `F-11`; `§6` `PAR-11`). **A diff that carries a lock reading through a new member, a new token, a new receipt form, a new union member, a new exported name or a new file is a COLLISION finding** (`§0A` item 6; `§1.3`).

4. **THE PAIRING RULE — THE FALSIFIABLE FORM OF THE WHOLE CLAUSE** (`T`, `CONTRACT`; this is the row `§5.6.1`'s row `10` drives). At EVERY probed instant of the window, the module's answers and the real path's bytes form **ONE AGREED PAIR**:
   - **BEFORE THE COMMIT LANDS:** `get()` = the **PRE-WRITE** record · `lastWriteReceipt()` = the last **LANDED** attempt's closed form (or `null`) · the real path's bytes = the **PRE-WRITE** record.
   - **AT AND AFTER THE COMMIT LANDING:** `get()` = the **CANDIDATE** · `lastWriteReceipt()` = `{status:'committed'}` · the real path's bytes = the **CANDIDATE**.
   - **AT A REFUSAL TERMINAL:** `get()` = the **PRE-WRITE** record · `lastWriteReceipt()` = `{status:'refused', reason:'write-failed'}` · the real path's bytes = the **PRE-WRITE** record.
   **A read that returns the PRE-WRITE record at an instant when the real path's bytes are already the CANDIDATE, OR the CANDIDATE at an instant when the real path's bytes are still the PRE-WRITE record, FAILS this clause** — and the FIRST of those two shapes is MEASURED on the as-landed bytes (`M`: the gate-5 blind row `SSD-G-81`, `docs/specs/secure-store-discipline-greens.md` `§5b` and `§6` finding 2 — at the post-rename directory `fsync` the re-entrant `get()` answered `token:'PRE'` while the real path carried `token:'DIRFSYNC'`).

5. **THE ORDER CONSEQUENCE — THIS CLAUSE ANSWERS THE RESIDUAL, AND IT IS A HOST OBLIGATION** (`A`, `CONTRACT`; `L`). **The record's advance MUST land AT the commit — inside the write's own window — so that item 4's pair holds at the post-rename probe.** The as-landed shape advances the record AFTER `persist()` returns (`L` `src/main/security-store.ts:293`'s `persist(` call and `:297`'s `if (staged.status === 'committed') current = candidate`) and therefore answers the **PRE-WRITE** record at that probe: **that shape is FAILING under this clause, and the answer to the residual is AFFIRMATIVE — THE ORDER IS REQUIRED, not merely unobservable.** **PRECEDENCE, STATED SO NO CLAUSE IS READ AGAINST ANOTHER (the point of this item):** where this subsection, `§0A` item 2's ORDER sentence and `§2.1` item 3 steps 4/5 differ — **(i) `§2.6` GOVERNS on WHETHER the advance lands at the commit (it DOES); (ii) `§0A` item 2 GOVERNS on WHAT A POST-RENAME DIRECTORY-`fsync` FAILURE DOES (the write is `committed`, the record stays advanced, nothing rolls back — UNCHANGED by this amendment); (iii) `§2.1` item 3 step 5's OPERATIVE READING is QUALIFIED IN EFFECT to its ORDER+OUTCOME half** — its *"NOT SEPARATELY OBSERVABLE"* claim is refuted by `SSD-G-81` and is no longer the operative reading, while that step's own order (the record advances only after a successful rename, and a failure of that step is not a refusal) STANDS. **No as-filed byte of either cell is rewritten: the dated notes beside `§0A` item 2 and `§2.1` item 3 step 5 carry this pointer.**
   **THE MECHANISM SPACE — TWO ADMISSIBLE SHAPES, BOTH INSIDE `§5.1` ITEM 1'S EXISTING EDIT SET** (`T`). **(h-i) a MODULE-INTERNAL COMMIT-LANDING SINK:** `set()` hands `persist()` a second, module-internal argument — the landing sink — invoked **EXACTLY ONCE** at the attempt's terminal: at the successful `renameSync` with `{status:'committed'}`, where it ALSO advances `current`; and at the caught failure with `{status:'refused', reason:'write-failed'}`, advancing nothing. **`persist()` still READS NO CLOSURE STATE and is still never given the closure's `current`** (`§0A` item 3; `§6` `PAR-7`'s OUTSIDE): the record state is touched by the **CALLER's** own closure, not by `persist()`, so `§2.1` item 4's declared shape is read as *"pure in its INPUTS"* — which `(h-i)` preserves (its dated note records the reading). **(h-ii) THE COMMIT POINT SEPARATED AT THE WRITE PATH'S BOUNDARY:** the `renameSync` and the directory `fsync` are separated so that the advance sits between them, inside the write path. **THE INVARIANTS EITHER SHAPE MUST HOLD (a row may assert them):** **(I-10-a)** the advance happens **IFF the `renameSync` succeeded**, at the first instant it did — never before a successful rename, never on a refusal, never after `persist()` has returned; **(I-10-b)** the record's answer and the receipt's answer **FLIP AT THE SAME TERMINAL** (item 4's pair is answered together, never one before the other). **Whichever shape lands is annotated beside `§6` `PAR-7` and `§2.1` item 4 in the same pass that lands it; a shape that reads the closure's `current` inside `persist()` remains OUTSIDE the declared shape and FAILS `P-M-SM-2`'s attempt (1).**

6. **TOTALITY AND NON-BLOCKING** (`T`, `CONTRACT`; `§2.3` item 1; `§8` item 1). **The lock is a DECLARED READING, not a concurrency primitive: it NEVER BLOCKS, NEVER WAITS, NEVER SPINS, NEVER QUEUES, NEVER RE-ENTERS and NEVER THROWS.** No member may suspend or wait for the in-flight attempt to finish: **a read that does not return is not an answer, and a row whose instrument cannot bound a read's return FAILS.** Every in-window read answers from item 3's vocabulary, synchronously, inside its own call.

7. **RE-ENTRANCY, COVERED EXPLICITLY** (`T`, `CONTRACT`; `M` for the witness). In a stock process no read can be issued inside the window — the module is synchronous and its write path calls nothing that could read the surface — **so the clause's observable sites are the write path's OWN filesystem effects, read through an instrumented `node:fs` (a mock, a shim, or a hook the harness installs: the same class of instrument the landed `P-M-SM-1`/`P-M-SM-2` rows and the gate-5 blind set already use).** Three in-window read shapes are NAMED — **(a) a read issued from inside the attempt's own `mkdirSync`/`writeFileSync`/`fsyncSync`/`renameSync`/`rmSync` path; (b) a read issued by a hook the write path invokes; (c) a read issued by the write path itself** — and **all three are in-window reads, answered by item 3 exactly like any other read: no special case, no exemption, no "internal" reading.** **The module's own write path MUST NOT consult the read surface while its window is open: it reads only its own LOCALS** (the candidate and the argument it was given — `§0A` item 3). **The named witness is `SSD-G-81`:** its post-rename directory-`fsync` probe is a row of THIS clause from now on, whose expected reading is the **CANDIDATE** with `{status:'committed'}`, and whose as-landed reading (`token:'PRE'`) is the **FAILING** shape.

8. **WHAT THIS CLAUSE DOES NOT DO AND DOES NOT CLAIM** (`T`, `NEGATIVE`). It adds **no rendered surface, no element, no text, no class, no slot content, no MCP surface, no IPC route, no export, no receipt form, no reason token, no `SecuritySettings` member, no persisted file, no union member and no second writer**; it changes **no** boot read, **no** `sanitize()` coercion, **no** group/`enabled` semantics and **no** pane flow; it makes **no timing claim and no concurrency claim** beyond the attempt's own step order (`§1.4` item 2); and it is **main-process module behaviour (`[H]`/`[T]`) — never an APP claim.** **Its red set is `§4.1` item 3, its register row is `§5.6.1`'s row `10`, its state row is `§2.4` `R-7`, its behaviour rows are `§3.1` `M-8` / `§3.2` `F-13` / `§3.3` `I-10`, and its `§7.1` re-check is `§5.2` item 6.** **NO DECLARED PARAMETER IS INTRODUCED BY THIS CLAUSE, so `§6` owes it NO new row (`§0B` item 4 — stated so the omission is DECLARED rather than silent):** the lock's inputs are the ALREADY-DECLARED ones (`opts.path`, the `set()` patch, the attempt's own steps), and the two `§6` rows it touches (`PAR-7`, `PAR-10`) carry DATED NOTES BESIDE them instead of new rows — because neither parameter's domain, unit, referent or evaluator changes; **a pass that adds a parameter, an option, a hook or a seam for the lock is adding a declared surface and must say so as a scope change, not smuggle it in as a note** (`§0A` item 6).

## 3. Behaviour (every state / fail-state)

### 3.1 Valid / happy states

| id | State | Trigger (exact) | Required behaviour | Pinned by | Layer |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **M-1** | **A representable write-through lands and the record advances at the commit point** | `store.set({token:'abc'})` on a fresh store at a writable path | admission passes; the candidate is `{token:'abc', enabled:['read','dispatch']}`; the file's bytes are the candidate; `get()` answers the candidate; `lastWriteReceipt()` answers `{status:'committed'}`; **no `${path}.tmp` remains** | `§2.1` item 3, `§2.4` `R-6`; `P-O1-TP-1`, `P-M-SM-1` (d) | `[H]`/`[T]` |
| **M-2** | **The ordered advance at the four failure-free steps** | `set({maxJournalLength: 50.7})` | admission passes; the candidate's cap is the floored `50`; the record advances **after** the rename and **before** the directory `fsync`; the receipt is committed | `§2.1` item 3, `§0A` item 2; `P-M-SM-1` (a) + (d) | `[H]`/`[T]` |
| **M-3** | **`lastWriteReceipt()` hands out a detached copy — twice** | `set({token:'t'})`; then read the receipt twice; then mutate the first copy | `null` before the attempt; after it: two calls answer two **different** objects, deep-equal; the mutation of the first copy does not appear on the second call and does not move the tier | `§2.5` items 1/4; `P-O3-IM-2` | `[T]` |
| **M-4** | **`get()` hands out a deep detached record** | `get()`; mutate the returned record's `enabled` array and, if the shape gained one, any nested member | the next `get()` answers the live record unchanged; the returned object is not the tier's own record; the copy's prototype is `null` | `§2.5` items 1/2; `P-O3-IM-2` | `[T]` |
| **M-5** | **The documented coercions still apply inside the admission's boundary** | `set({token:'  a  '})` (a non-empty string, kept verbatim) · `set({groups:['code','code','nope']})` (dedup + filter) · `set({maxJournalLength: 50.7})` (floored) · `set({token:''})` (cleared) · `set({maxJournalLength: 0})` (cleared) · `set({maxJournalLength: null})` (cleared) · `set({})` (absent members keep the current value) | every one is ADMITTED, answers `committed`, and lands the declared post-state in-memory AND in the file | `§2.2` item 3, `§2.1` item 3; `P-O2-TP-1` | `[H]`/`[T]` |
| **M-6** | **The read-only patch shape is admitted and keeps the record** | `set({token: undefined})` (the absent marker) | admitted; `committed`; the record's `token` keeps its current value while `maxJournalLength`/`enabled` are untouched too | `§2.2` item 3; `P-O2-TP-1` | `[T]` |
| **M-7** | **The first-run default's boot chain is preserved** | boot with a missing file · then with a corrupt file · then with the path a DIRECTORY | each answers `{token:null, enabled:['read','dispatch'], maxJournalLength: undefined}`; **nothing throws**; the constructor writes no file | `§2.1` item 2, `§2.3` item 7(i)/(ii); `P-O2-TP-1` | `[H]`/`[T]` |
| **M-8** | **The write lock's paired answers at the window's two halves** *(APPENDED `2026-10-11`)* | drive a COMMITTING write with an instrumented `node:fs` and read the pair at the PRE-RENAME tmp `fsync`, then at the POST-RENAME directory `fsync` | at the first: `get()` = the **PRE-WRITE** record, `lastWriteReceipt()` = the last landed closed form (`null` on a first attempt), the real path's bytes = the **PRE-WRITE** record; at the second: `get()` = the **CANDIDATE**, `lastWriteReceipt()` = `{status:'committed'}`, the real path's bytes = the **CANDIDATE**; **nothing throws, blocks or fails to return in either reading** | `§2.6` items 2/3/4/6; `P-M-SM-3` | `[H]`/`[T]` |

### 3.2 Documented fail-states / non-happy states

| id | Fail-state | Trigger (exact) | Required behaviour | Pinned by | Layer |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **F-1** | **A refused persist rolls the record back — the headline** | force the persist to fail (the parent a regular FILE; the parent `0500`; the target a DIRECTORY) and call `set({token:'x', maxJournalLength:55})` | the receipt is `{status:'refused', reason:'write-failed'}`; **`get()` answers the PRE-WRITE record member for member** (NOT `x`, NOT `55`); the file's bytes are byte-identical to the pre-write bytes; **no throw** | `§2.1` item 3, `§2.3` items 2/3/5, `§2.4` `R-2`/`R-4`; `P-O1-TP-1`, `P-O1-TP-1` | `[H]`/`[T]` |
| **F-2** | **An unrepresentable value is NOT accepted** | `set({maxJournalLength: Infinity})` · `set({token: BigInt(7)})` · `set({token: new Map()})` · `set({token: new Date()})` · `set({groups: [BigInt(7)]})` · `set({token: (()=>{})})` · a cyclic object on any member | **every one is REFUSED, whole-patch**: `{status:'refused', reason:'write-failed'}`; **the record does not move**; the file is untouched (no tmp created); **no `committed` receipt ever answers a discarded value** | `§2.2` items 1/3/4/5, `§2.4` `R-1`; `P-O2-IM-1`, `P-O2-TP-1` | `[H]`/`[T]` |
| **F-3** | **The `Infinity` divergence is CLOSED** | `set({maxJournalLength: Infinity})` at a writable path | refused; **the record's cap is whatever it was** (never `<Infinity>`); **the file's bytes carry no `null` cap injected by the refusal**; there is **no in-memory-versus-file divergence for a non-finite number**, because the value never enters either | `§2.2` items 1/3, `§2.3` item 7; `P-O2-IM-1` | `[H]`/`[T]` |
| **F-4** | **A prototype-poisoned patch value does not enter, and cannot pollute** | `set({token: JSON.parse('{"__proto__":{"polluted":true}}')})` | the value is an ordinary object and is thus NOT a `string` ⇒ **declared-domain refusal, whole patch**; `Object.prototype` is **unpolluted**; the record and the file are untouched | `§2.2` items 1/5, `§2.3` item 1; `P-O2-TP-1` | `[T]` |
| **F-5** | **A non-object patch is REFUSED, never thrown** | `set(null)` · `set(undefined)` · `set(7)` · `set('x')` · `set(()=>{})` | each answers the **whole-patch refusal** (`{status:'refused', reason:'write-failed'}`), the record unmoved, **NEVER a throw** — the landed `TypeError` on `set(null)` is CLOSED | `§2.3` item 1, `§2.4` `R-1`; `P-O2-TP-1`, `P-TP-2` | `[T]` |
| **F-6** | **The receipt is never a third token and never `undefined`** | any refusal · any commit | `reason` is **EXACTLY** `'write-failed'` on a refusal; the status is EXACTLY one of the two closed tokens; **no `reason` on a commit**; **no `null` after an attempt** | `§2.1` item 7, `§2.3` item 6; `P-TP-2` | `[H]`/`[T]` |
| **F-7** | **A throw from any declared member FAILS the totality rows** | the hostile filesystem (a tmp that names a DIRECTORY · a `renameSync` onto a directory · a `readFileSync` onto a directory at boot) and the hostile patch (a hostile proxy · a non-object · a cyclic value) | no declared member throws on any of them; the refused receipt (or the boot default) is the answer; **an error escaping `set()`/`persist()`/`createSecurityStore` is a FAILED row** | `§2.3` items 1/4, `§2.2` item 1; `P-TP-2`, `P-O2-TP-1` | `[H]`/`[T]` |
| **F-8** | **A live record advanced past a refusal FAILS the rollback invariant** | the implementation assigns the candidate before saving (today's `:138-139`) | the invariant row reddens: the live record must equal the durable record at every instant; **a rollback implemented as assign-then-restore FAILS**, even where the observable answers coincide | `§2.3` item 2; `P-O1-TP-1`, `P-M-SM-2` | `[H]`/`[T]` |
| **F-9** | **An admission refusal that touches the filesystem FAILS** | an unrepresentable patch, with the store's path instrumented | the invariant row reddens: no `${path}.tmp` may be created and no `writeFileSync`/`renameSync` may be called for a patch refused at admission | `§0A` item 3, `§2.2` item 5(c); `P-O2-IM-1`, `P-M-SM-1` (e) | `[T]` |
| **F-10** | **A shared receipt, or a `get()` alias, FAILS the clone property** | the implementation hands out its own receipt object or a live `enabled` array (today's `lastWriteReceipt`) | the clone rows redden: `a === b` must be `false` across two calls and a holder's mutation must be invisible on the next read | `§2.5` items 1/3/4; `P-O3-IM-2` | `[T]` |
| **F-11** | **A fourth value-returning member, or a renamed one, FAILS the census** | a diff that adds a declared member to the returned object, or renames one of the three | the census row reddens (`Object.keys(store)` must be set-equal to `["get","lastWriteReceipt","set"]`) | `§2.5` item 3; `P-O3-TP-1`'s part (a) | `[T]` (static) |
| **F-12** | **An edit to a frozen or out-of-scope file FAILS** | any byte of `src/renderer/store-core-graph.ts`/`store-graph-references.ts` moving; a `src/shared/**` edit; a `main.ts`/`preload.ts` edit; a new exported name in the module | the byte-pin row, the export census row and the diff-scope census redden — **a COLLISION finding** | `§0A` item 6, `§1.3` items 1/4/5/7, `§5.1`; `P-O3-TP-1`'s parts (b)/(c) | `[T]` (static) |
| **F-13** | **A READ THAT OBSERVES THE PRE-WRITE RECORD — OR THE CANDIDATE — FROM INSIDE THE WINDOW FAILS THE PAIRING** *(APPENDED `2026-10-11`; the lock's own fail-state)* | **(a)** the as-landed shape (the advance AFTER `persist()` returns): a re-entrant `get()` at the POST-rename directory `fsync` answers the **PRE-WRITE** record while the real path's bytes already carry the **CANDIDATE** — the measured `SSD-G-81` reading; **(b)** an assign-early shape: a re-entrant `get()` at the PRE-rename tmp `fsync` answers the **CANDIDATE** while the real path's bytes still carry the **PRE-WRITE** record; **(c)** `lastWriteReceipt()` answering the IN-FLIGHT attempt's own form before that attempt's terminal has landed | the lock rows redden: the pair must **AGREE at every probed instant** (`§2.6` item 4), the receipt must be **WITHHELD** until its terminal (`§2.6` item 3(b)), the advance must land **AT** the commit (`§2.6` item 5's `I-10-a`/`I-10-b`), and a read that blocks, waits or throws fails outright (`§2.6` item 6) | `§2.6` items 3/4/5/6; `P-M-SM-3` | `[H]`/`[T]` |

### 3.3 Invariants that hold in every state

1. **`I-1` — LIVE STATE EQUALS DURABLE STATE**: the live record advances IFF a save succeeded; after every attempt, `get()`'s answer equals what the file's bytes carry — the pre-write record on a refusal, the candidate on a commit (`§2.3` items 2/7; `P-O1-TP-1`).
2. **`I-2` — NO VALUE THE TIER CANNOT PERSIST AS JSON IS ACCEPTED**: every accepted patch's members are JSON-representable and every accepted member's value is preserved (the documented coercions aside, and each of those keeps the member's declared form) (`§2.2`; `P-O2-IM-1`).
3. **`I-3` — NO `committed` RECEIPT ANSWERS A DISCARDED VALUE** (`§2.2` item 4; `P-O2-TP-1`).
4. **`I-4` — NO DECLARED MEMBER THROWS, EVER**: for any patch shape, any filesystem state and any hostile value, the answer is a value — a receipt, a record — and never an exception (`§2.3` item 1; `P-TP-2`).
5. **`I-5` — THE RECEIPT IS TOTAL AND CLOSED**: exactly one of the two landed forms answers every attempt; `null` only before the first attempt (`§2.1` item 7; `P-TP-2`).
6. **`I-6` — THE READ SURFACE RETURNS DETACHED COPIES AT EVERY DEPTH, over EXACTLY the three measured members** (`§2.5`; `P-O3-IM-2`, `P-O3-TP-1`'s part (a)).
7. **`I-7` — THE FILE AT THE REAL PATH IS NEVER TORN AND IS NEVER ADVANCED BY A REFUSED WRITE**, and no `${path}.tmp` is ever parsed as the record (`§2.3` items 3/4; `P-O2-IM-2`).
8. **`I-8` — THE STORE'S CLOSED VOCABULARIES AND FROZEN BYTES ARE UNMOVED**: the receipt's two forms and one token (`§2.1` item 7), the store's 16-member refusal union, the `secure.*` refusal's single site, the module's export census, and the frozen store module's file hashes (`§0A` item 6, `§1.3`; `P-O3-TP-1`'s parts (b)/(c)).
9. **`I-9` — THE TIER'S REACH AND THE PERSISTED SET ARE UNMOVED**: no new reach, no name-addressing, exactly the two persisted files, one writer (`§1.1` items 2/3).
10. **`I-10` — THE READS ARE LOCKED OUT UNTIL THE COMMIT LANDS** *(APPENDED `2026-10-11`)*: at every instant of a write's window the read surface answers from the **DURABLE** state — `§2.6` item 4's pair holds, the in-flight attempt contributes nothing to the read surface until its terminal lands, the advance lands at the commit itself (`I-10-a`/`I-10-b`), and the lock never blocks, waits, re-enters or throws (`§2.6` items 2/3/4/5/6; `P-M-SM-3`).
